# Campaign findings

## proposals-20260919T172712Z-8b7bda99

Source inspection sharpens the head idea's premise and should be recorded in prior-work.md: the incumbent sigma is `sigma_fraction * limiting_reach(pool)` where `limiting_reach` (sampling.py:326) is `max(min(-lo, hi), 1.0)` — the minimum of the two acquired half-extents. The symmetric truncated Gaussian is therefore pinned by the shorter side, so the longer coronal side's reach is discounted twice (once by truncation, once by the shared width). This is a structural asymmetry in the current sampler, independent of the value of the 0.867 fraction, and it is what distinguishes coronal-side-relative-draw from the already-trained common-factor sweeps. Caveat: the effect on realized exposure is bounded by discrete slice availability — targets are matched to existing candidate geometry via `geometry_groups` and a linear-sum assignment (sampling.py:513-534), so a wider anterior target distribution only changes exposure where anterior slices exist. The developer must measure realized depth distributions in the CPU replay rather than infer them from the target distribution. Campaign ownership note for the registry: both imported ideas are now owned by this campaign; docs/backlog/untried-ideas.md entries are read-only provenance and must not be launched as duplicate general-backlog arms.

Queue was empty with no reserved head, so both imported proposals are accepted and ranked freely; neither duplicates a rejected or blocked direction (per-type centre offsets, common-factor sigma sweeps, fixed-total reallocations, repeated-series share, side-conditioned sagittal sampling all stay out of the queue).

Head: coronal-side-relative-draw. It has the clearest mechanism-to-evidence link and is the only proposal that changes distribution shape rather than scale. Source inspection confirms the opportunity is real and not a restatement of the sigma sweep: sampling.py:326 `limiting_reach` returns `max(min(-lo, hi), 1.0)`, i.e. the single sigma feeding `target_depths` is set by the *shorter* acquired side, so the longer coronal side (about 59.0 mm anterior vs 45.6 mm posterior) is systematically under-weighted by construction. A two-sided width is a distinct mechanism from any scalar on 0.867. Implementation cost is low and localized: `interval_log_mass` and the `truncnorm.ppf` branch in `target_depths` (sampling.py:333-367), with the plane available via the existing `kind` argument to `select_triplets`; no new decoding, precomputation, or data.

Second: geometry-informed-plane-widths. Motivated and cheap, but its upside is less certain — its supporting evidence is cache-only and the relative-width result showed cache inference did not predict the training effect. It also carries the researcher's own flagged risk of collapsing into a scalar sweep, which the campaign forbids. Ranked second rather than parked: a single justified plane policy derived from measured per-plane reach is a genuine shape change, and it is cheap to attempt after the coronal result clarifies whether shape changes move training at all. Park it if development shows it reduces to enumerating multipliers.

Queue depth is 2, below the refill threshold of 4, so a researcher refill to 5 should run concurrently with development. Refill must deduplicate against mechanism_ids coronal-side-relative-distribution and plane-specific-sampling-shape, and must not requeue the blocked or previously-resolved directions listed above.

Developer assignment (coronal-side-relative-draw), fixed constraints and completion condition:
- Starting snapshot: the sealed baseline source at outputs/autoresearch/triplet-sampling/baseline/source; incumbent is historical-control, run overnight-20260919T120130Z-i2oqlnls/runs/00-control, predictions sha256 6f8f0ef5…, best macro AUC 0.894037. Reference recipe is sigma fraction 0.867, not the superseded 0.76 prose.
- Mechanism: replace the symmetric truncated Gaussian for Coronal pools with a two-sided draw whose per-side widths derive from that side's own physical reach, keeping the fitted physical centre. The allocation of probability mass across the two sides must be an explicit, stated choice, not an accident of the reach ratio. Axial and Sagittal keep the incumbent policy. Verify the anterior/posterior sign convention and the discrete support-matching in `geometry_groups`/`linear_sum_assignment` before claiming which side actually gains exposure — a target shift that no candidate slice can satisfy changes nothing.
- Fixed constraints (gates.py enforces these): training_triplets exactly 938460, training_visits exactly 35030, validation_triplets exactly 24188 per pass, tolerance zero; 10 epochs; 4380 optimizer steps; validation multiplier 1; fold 0 of all_5fold_v3 only; cohort sha256 unchanged for both phases; total wall clock ≤ 1785 s including preparation and CPU comparison. Config changes confined to data.triplet_sampling.* (plus the logistical keys); any other recipe key fails the gate.
- Validation identity: deterministic selection must not depend on the training seed. If the deterministic validation selection changes at all relative to the incumbent, record the new selection identity explicitly — the accounting requires one stable selection_sha256 across all ten validation rows.
- Stochasticity: training selection must still vary across epochs (changed_training_studies must be non-empty). Draw and record a fresh seed at launch; configs/training.yaml seed 42 must be replaced.
- Latitude: side-mass normalization, treatment of degenerate or single-sided pools, and code organization are the developer's call, with the reasoning recorded. No parameter sweep, no second arm.
- Completion condition: a diff against parent_source plus a recorded source and resolved configuration, and a full-schedule CPU replay showing realized counts equal to the sealed limits at zero tolerance and the two-sided widths taking effect on coronal pools, ready for orchestrator review before any supervisor launch request.

## review-20260919T173926Z-9bec7252

Durable findings to record in prior-work.md.

1. The incumbent coronal draw is doubly pinned by the shorter side, and the fix is exactly expressible as a two-piece normal. sigma = sigma_fraction * limiting_reach(pool) with limiting_reach = max(min(-lo, hi), 1.0) (sampling.py:326). The candidate replaces this for physical Coronal pools with per-side sigma from that side's own union-support reach and allocates mass proportional to each side's sigma, which is the unique continuous-at-zero, unit-normalized two-piece normal. Verified numerically on the candidate module: unit total mass, negative-side mass exactly sigma_lo/(sigma_lo+sigma_hi), exact self-CDF inversion, and exact degeneration to the incumbent when the two sides are equal. That last property is useful for future work: any per-side width policy can be checked for incumbent-compatibility by setting the widths equal.

2. Quantified exposure shift for the representative coronal pool (support -59.0/+45.6 mm, fraction 0.867): anterior sigma 39.5 -> 51.2 mm, anterior target mass 0.535 -> 0.564, posterior mass 0.465 -> 0.436. So "matched to acquired side extents" widens and up-weights the anterior side and slightly de-weights the posterior side. This is worth recording because the idea's motivating observation was posterior (Baker's cyst, posterior horn): the reach-proportional normalization moves mass in the opposite direction from that motivation. A null or negative result therefore does not refute asymmetric coronal sampling in general; it tests reach-proportional allocation specifically. A posterior-favoring allocation with the same two-sided widths remains a distinct, untested normalization choice, but it must not be spawned as a sweep arm of this idea.

3. Realized exposure remains bounded by discrete slice availability. Targets are matched to existing candidate geometry through geometry_groups and linear_sum_assignment (sampling.py:513-534), which the candidate leaves byte-identical. This gives a structural guarantee of exact count preservation (count = min(budget, len(groups)) is untouched), and it also means target-distribution shifts only translate into exposure where candidate slices exist. Realized depth histograms from the CPU replay, not target distributions, are the right evidence for any shape-change idea in this campaign.

4. Methodological note for future candidates: scalar scipy.stats.truncnorm.ppf costs about 0.081 ms per call on this machine versus 0.0034 ms per element when vectorized, a ~24x per-element penalty. Any sampler change that replaces a vectorized ppf with a Python loop should budget this explicitly against the 1,785 s ceiling. For this candidate the exposure is ~290k coronal calls, ~22 s CPU, ~1-2 s wall across 16 workers, which is affordable; a change touching all planes would be roughly three times that and closer to material.

5. Provenance constraint discovered during development: src/knee_abnormality/data/triplet_sampling/ is not listed in identity.PREPROCESSING_SOURCES, so sampler logic placed there would escape the preprocessing source hash. Campaign sampler changes must therefore live in data/preprocessing/sampling.py (or another listed module) to keep provenance fidelity, since identity.py is not editable by candidate agents. The candidate left an empty triplet_sampling/ directory behind; it is inert but should be removed. Relatedly, any SAMPLING_IDENTITY edit makes preprocessing_identity differ, so comparisons for such candidates need --allowed-input-identity-differences preprocessing_identity as a standing expectation rather than a per-case surprise.

6. Campaign ownership, carried forward unchanged: both imported ideas are owned by this campaign; docs/backlog/untried-ideas.md entries are read-only provenance and must not be launched as duplicate general-backlog arms. The old 0.008 heuristic remains superseded by the sealed paired-bootstrap acceptance rule.

Review event: approve the coronal-side-relative-draw candidate and keep it as the reserved head; geometry-informed-plane-widths remains second, unchanged by this evidence. No proposals to accept, nothing parked.

Approval basis (independent verification, not the author's assertion):
- Scope: diff -rq against parent_source shows only src/knee_abnormality/data/preprocessing/sampling.py differs. No config/CLI keys added; configs/training.yaml untouched (seed 2583306572 present, supervisor still draws a fresh seed at launch). One inert artifact: an empty src/knee_abnormality/data/triplet_sampling/ directory with no files, not imported and not in identity.py PREPROCESSING_SOURCES, so it cannot affect behavior or sources_sha256. Recommend deleting it before launch as cleanliness only.
- Math, executed against the candidate module with PYTHONPATH pinned to the candidate source: total mass over (-1e7,1e7) = 0.9999999999999997; negative-side mass 0.5644983461962510 vs analytic sigma_lo/(sigma_lo+sigma_hi) 0.5644983461962514; straddling interval log-mass equals logsumexp of its two halves (0.4182570107700266 vs 0.4182570107700265); split_normal_ppf is monotone, stays inside [lo,hi], and inverts its own truncated CDF exactly (f=0.1/0.5/0.9 -> cdf 0.100000/0.500000/0.900000); with sigma_lo==sigma_hi it matches sigma*truncnorm.ppf to 1e-9, i.e. it degenerates to the incumbent. One-sided intervals route to the single-piece branches correctly, and the union_support floor max(reach,1.0) mirrors limiting_reach, so degenerate pools cannot produce a non-finite or non-positive sigma (the guard was extended to check both tuple entries).
- Coverage/exploitation: for the representative coronal pool (support -59.0/+45.6 mm, fraction 0.867) posterior target mass moves 0.465 -> 0.436 and anterior sigma widens 39.5 -> 51.2 mm. Both acquired sides retain substantial mass and central coverage is preserved; peripheral posterior support is retained but modestly reduced. The side-mass choice (mass proportional to each side's reach, the continuity-preserving two-piece normal) is one explicit stated normalization, matching the idea title, with no second arm and no sweep. Caveat to carry into interpretation: the implementation shifts mass slightly away from the posterior side that the Baker's-cyst/posterior-horn observation motivated, so a null or negative result does not by itself refute asymmetric coronal draws.
- Count accounting: study_budgets, geometry_groups, largest_remainder and the linear_sum_assignment supplier/assignment logic are byte-identical to parent_source. The change is confined to target_depths and the sigma computation, which relocate targets within already-existing discrete candidate slices; count = min(budget, len(groups)) is therefore structurally invariant, consistent with the exact 220-triplet/8-visit match on the developer's subset.
- Validation independence and identity: sigma derives solely from pool geometry (union_support), never from the training seed or any RNG, and the validation percentile path is unchanged, so deterministic validation remains seed-independent. Deterministic selection does change relative to the incumbent (expected and permitted); the developer recorded the new identity on the subset (candidate 67fab5..., baseline 905de0...). Training selection remains stochastic across epochs.
- Leakage: no label, fold, cohort, grouping, scoring or checkpoint-selection code touched; no validation-derived feedback; no persistent precomputation or extra decode pass.
- Added cost measured, not assumed: split_normal_ppf replaces one vectorized truncnorm.ppf with a per-element loop. Benchmark on this machine gives ~0.081 ms per scalar call vs ~0.0034 ms per element vectorized. Coronal is ~31% of the 938,460 training triplets (max_images 15+15 of 96), i.e. ~290k extra scalar calls, ~22 s CPU in total, ~1-2 s wall across 16 dataloader workers. That sits well inside the 233 s headroom between the 1,551.5 s historical control and the 1,785 s ceiling, so this does not warrant the one allowed repair. Vectorizing the two branches with boolean masks would remove the margin question and can be folded in opportunistically, but is not required for approval.

## proposals-20260919T174356Z-274f09e4

Durable findings from this proposals event, for prior-work.md.

1. Cross-slot depth duplication is the largest unexploited weakness measured so far, and it concentrates in the scored bag. On the fold-0 token cache (outputs/analysis/t/token-cache-20260917/, deterministic x1 at budgets 6/6/5/5/5/5) the share of one contrast slot's selected middles within 5 mm of a partner-slot middle is axial 0.913 (179 studies), coronal 0.951 (674), sagittal 0.968 (803), median nearest cross-slot distance 0.00 mm. Under training jitter the same statistic falls to 0.581/0.561/0.556. Confirmed not to be a bookkeeping artifact: 0 of 1,656 plane instances share a series between slots, so this is co-prescribed geometry being sampled twice by two independent `select_triplets` calls that share a study centre and near-identical support. Consequence: a two-contrast plane contributes ~5-6 distinct depths to a study bag, not ~10-12, and the redundancy is worst in the deterministic selection that is actually evaluated.

2. Verified source facts underpinning three of the four accepted ideas (checked directly against the sealed baseline source, not taken from the report). (a) Training jitter is `percentiles = (arange(count) + rng("targets").random(count)) / count` and the epoch enters the sampler only through `operation_seed`, so cross-epoch coupling is currently pure independence — the campaign's named "evolution across training" axis is entirely untouched. (b) The supplier draw is asymmetric between phases: training uses `choices[supplier_rng.integers(len(choices))]`, validation uses `max(choices, key=lambda s: entitlement[s] - assigned[s])`, and `entitlement` is already accumulated as exact Fractions on both paths but discarded in training. The uniform draw matches entitlement only in expectation, so within an epoch a repeated-type slot's whole budget can land on a superseded retake or reformat. (c) `preparation.py:125-130` raises "Triplet slices must be consecutive in the indexed series", which is a hard blocker for any change to triplet channel stride.

3. Provenance map, extended from the previous review's finding. identity.PREPROCESSING_SOURCES lists data/dataset.py, data/preprocessing/{dicom,exclusions,filtering,geometry,intensity,preparation,sampling,series,spatial}.py, modeling/{augmentation,model,spatial_tokens}.py. Two consequences worth carrying: data/triplet_sampling/ remains outside the hash (sampler logic must not live there), but data/preprocessing/preparation.py is inside it, so a candidate that must relax the consecutive-slice invariant can do so without losing provenance fidelity. Any such candidate's comparison needs --allowed-input-identity-differences preprocessing_identity.

4. Through-plane geometry is unstandardized while in-plane geometry is not. In-plane is resampled to 0.625 mm at 224 px; a triplet is three consecutive slices, so its physical extent is whatever the acquisition prescribed. Measured per pool on fold-0: median candidate gap 3.32 mm (p1 0.03, p5 0.61, p95 5.07), giving triplet spans from ~0.06 mm to ~18 mm, with 261 of 4,368 pools (6.0%) below 1 mm median spacing and therefore feeding the backbone three near-identical channels. Median realized span is 7.11 mm, the natural anchor for any standardization. Caveat recorded with the number: the p1 0.03 mm cases may be broken position fields rather than fine acquisitions and must be inspected before being treated as recoverable.

5. Directions now closed by measurement, not opinion — do not requeue. (a) Intra-pool overlap/repulsion is dead: 0 adjacent-middle pairs and 69 gap-2 pairs across 18,555 deterministic selections, i.e. 69 of 55,665 channel appearances redundant (0.12%), against mean pool capacity 36.3 versus budget 5-6. Equal-mass stratification already does the work the retired hard non-overlap constraint attempted, and that constraint cost budget in 231 of 595 groups. (b) Capacity-recovery and shortfall reallocation are contract-blocked, not merely unpromising: raising realized totals fails the zero-tolerance gate, and the count-neutral remainder is exactly the fixed-total reallocation family already measured as tie-or-negative. (c) Role-split distributions (train wide / validate narrow, or any training-only shape) are argued against by the relative-width decomposition's matched-condition effect: each model prefers the sampler it was trained under by about 0.0006 (+0.00059 new, -0.00061 control). Candidates should change both sides together and record the new validation identity.

6. Standing evidence limitation for this campaign. Every cache-derived number above comes from the fold-0 *validation* cohort (904 studies, 4,368 pools) built under the sigma-40 recipe with fraction 0.867 applied post hoc; the training cohort has never been measured directly, and the cross-epoch and cross-slot simulations use nearest-depth matching that ignores the supplier-preferring linear-sum assignment. The control run's artifacts record counts, not realized depth histograms, so there is no per-epoch exposure measurement from the incumbent itself. Every shape- or coverage-motivated candidate must therefore re-derive its realized effect from the full-schedule CPU replay rather than cite these figures as outcomes.

7. Retained but unqueued idea, recorded so it is not lost: bimodal / compartment-weighted sagittal draw. Sagittal is the only axis whose measured total utility favours the flanks over the centre (flanks minus centre +0.126, against -0.634 coronal and -1.099 axial), peaking near -31.5 and +31.5 mm against a lower centre, while the incumbent truncated Gaussian (realized sagittal sigma 39.0 mm) places about 28% less mass at that peak than at the centre. No width setting can express this because the Gaussian is unimodal, which is why it is not a restatement of the sigma sweep. The reflection-symmetry objection that blocks side-conditioned sagittal sampling does not apply: a symmetric two-lobe draw is the correct response under both readings of the trough, and p=0.5 reflection augmentation already symmetrizes the model's view, so no knee-side plumbing or mirrored re-export is needed. Deliberately kept separate from geometry-informed-plane-widths rather than folded into it.

Proposals event: accept all four proposals, queue depth goes 1 -> 5 (at target). Reserved head unchanged, nothing parked, no refill needed.

Head (reserved, not displaceable): geometry-informed-plane-widths, status developing, experiment geometry-informed-plane-widths-20260919T174231Z-cb932a0e. New evidence reranks only the unclaimed tail. Its standing park condition is unchanged: park it if development reduces it to enumerating plane multipliers rather than one justified policy.

2. cross-slot-band-interleaving. Ranked above the researcher's own second choice on potential and learning value. It is the only proposal that changes the content of the scored deterministic bag rather than its sequence or its provenance, and the measured weakness is the largest in the refill (0.913/0.951/0.968 of one slot's selected middles within 5 mm of a partner-slot middle, median nearest distance 0.00 mm, with 0 of 1,656 plane instances sharing a series so it is not a bookkeeping artifact). Count-preserving by construction: each slot keeps its own pool, support, sigma and budget; only the sub-band lattice it draws from changes. The sign is genuinely uncertain (co-located T1/PD and fluid triplets may be complementary), but both outcomes are informative about a mechanism family with no queue representation, and cost is a lattice offset. Two things the developer must settle before claiming an effect: partner presence must come from the already-computed `_slot_sources` and be fixed by config order, never by seed; and realized exposure must be read from CPU-replay depth histograms, not target distributions, because `geometry_groups` + `linear_sum_assignment` (sampling.py:513-534) bound any retarget to existing candidate slices.

3. run-level-stratified-jitter. The cleanest contract profile in the refill and near-zero implementation risk: verified in source that the per-epoch draw is `(arange(count) + rng("targets").random(count)) / count` with the epoch entering only through `operation_seed`, so replacing independent per-epoch jitter with a run-level permutation of sub-strata leaves the per-epoch marginal bit-identical and leaves the deterministic validation path (midpoints, no jitter, no epoch) untouched — the incumbent validation `selection_sha256` is expected to hold exactly, and that expectation is itself a check. Ranked below cross-slot because it changes the correlation between bags rather than any bag, so its expected magnitude is smaller. The motivating +14.7% distinct-middles number is a nearest-depth approximation on the validation-cohort token cache and must be re-derived in the replay. Stratification depth E belongs under `data.triplet_sampling.*`, not `trainer.max_epochs`; wrap-around must keep a resumed run reproducible.

4. entitlement-balanced-supplier-draw. Verified in source: training takes `choices[supplier_rng.integers(len(choices))]` while validation takes `max(choices, key=entitlement - assigned)`, with `entitlement` already computed as exact Fractions on both paths and discarded in training. So the train/validation supplier-policy mismatch is real and the fix is confined to one loop. Ranked fourth on reach, not on quality: only 15.1% of pools (660 of 4,368) can move at all, and the previously measured share deviation (4.53 vs 3.30 points, under a different sampler) sizes the correction as small. One factor only — balance alone, no quality weighting on top — so the arm isolates the supplier lottery. Note for development: `rng("band_order")` is near-inert in training today (it permutes accumulation order while the choice stays uniform) and is the natural carrier for a randomized start.

5. physical-span-standardized-triplets. The most original mechanism in the refill and the only one that changes what a triplet is rather than where it goes, but ranked last on reach and readiness: it bites on the ~6% of pools below 1 mm median spacing, and it is the only proposal blocked on a scope decision. Scope decision, made now so it is not a surprise at assignment: widening the developer edit set to `data/preprocessing/preparation.py` is granted when this idea reaches development, limited to the consecutive-slice invariant (preparation.py:125-130, verified present) and its immediate callers. This is safe for provenance — `preparation.py` is already listed in identity.PREPROCESSING_SOURCES, so the change stays inside the preprocessing source hash and the comparison will need `--allowed-input-identity-differences preprocessing_identity` as a standing expectation. Two preconditions before any implementation: inspect the p1 0.03 mm pools to establish whether they are fine acquisitions or broken position fields (do not assume they are recoverable), and confirm in the CPU replay that unique-decode counts hold, since decoding is the runtime-sensitive part against the 1,785 s ceiling. First arm uses a fixed stride anchored to the cohort median realized span (7.11 mm), matching how 0.867 was anchored; no jitter, no sweep.

Surplus retained, not queued: the bimodal / compartment-weighted sagittal draw described in the researcher's exhausted_directions. It is evidence-backed (sagittal is the only axis whose measured utility favours flanks over centre, +0.126 against -0.634 coronal and -1.099 axial, peaking near ±31.5 mm, which no unimodal truncated Gaussian can express) and the reflection-symmetry objection that blocks the side-conditioned sagittal big bet does not apply. I decline the researcher's suggestion to fold it into geometry-informed-plane-widths: that head's mechanism is per-plane width, folding in a modality change would either mutate a reserved head's mechanism or create a second arm, both of which the campaign forbids. Hold it as a distinct proposal for the next refill, after coronal-side-relative-draw reports.

No refill requested: queue depth is 5, at target and above the threshold of 4. The at-most-one-in-flight rule stands — no idea below the head enters development until the head's launch removes it.

## review-20260919T175007Z-9a9452dc

Durable findings to record in prior-work.md.

1. The per-plane width policy is recoverable in closed form, which is what distinguishes it from a multiplier sweep and is worth recording for any future per-plane work. Widths proportional to the documented usable half-reach ratio 1.35 : 1.00 : 1.12 (axial 61.5 / coronal 45.6 / sagittal 51.0 mm), normalized so the cohort mean width is preserved at 39.967 mm, give k = 34.553 and exact target widths 46.65 / 34.55 / 38.70 mm, i.e. multipliers 1.0343 / 0.9652 / 0.9923 on the realized 45.1 / 35.8 / 39.0 mm. The docs' rounded targets 47 / 35 / 39 mm correspond to multipliers 1.0421 / 0.9777 / 1.0000, the values implemented. The scale is therefore not a free parameter: it is pinned by mean preservation, exactly as sigma_fraction 0.867 was pinned to preserve the previous mean width.

2. Quantified consequence of the rounding, recorded so results are interpreted correctly: the rounded constants inflate all three widths by +0.76 / +1.29 / +0.78 percent relative to the mean-preserving derivation, leaving a +0.92% uniform scale drift (implied mean width 40.33 vs 39.97 mm). The arm is therefore a ratio change plus a small common-scale nudge, not a pure ratio change. Given the settled flatness of common-factor sigma sweeps, ~1% of scale is not a plausible driver of any observed effect, but a future exact-ratio variant would be a legitimately distinct measurement rather than a rerun.

3. Expected effect magnitude is intrinsically modest and this should temper interpretation of a null. The realized widths already partially track acquisition geometry: current axial:coronal width ratio is 45.1/35.8 = 1.260 against the geometry target 1.343, so the intervention moves that ratio by about 6.6%. The multiplier spread (max/min = 1.066) is much smaller than the geometry spread (1.35) precisely because limiting_reach already carries part of the per-plane signal into the common-fraction rule. An inconclusive interval here bounds the residual value of per-plane width shaping to a small effect; it does not test large per-plane reshaping.

4. Structural result reusable by every future shape-change candidate, now verified twice: in select_triplets, sigma is consumed only by target_depths and by the two metadata fields sampling_sigma_mm / sampling_sigma_fraction. It never enters geometry_groups, the support construction, study_budgets, largest_remainder, or the linear_sum_assignment supplier/assignment stage. Therefore any candidate that changes sigma alone is exactly count-preserving by construction (count = min(budget, len(groups)) is sigma-free) and satisfies the zero-tolerance gate structurally, while its realized exposure remains bounded by discrete slice availability. Corollary for verification: sampling_sigma_mm is the right metadata field to read back from the CPU replay to confirm a width policy took effect, because it now records the plane-adjusted value while sampling_sigma_fraction still records only the base 0.867.

5. Runtime contrast between the two shape candidates, worth carrying as a design preference. The coronal split-normal candidate replaced a vectorized truncnorm.ppf with a per-element Python loop, costing ~22 s CPU (~24x per-element penalty, benchmarked at 0.081 ms per scalar call vs 0.0034 ms per vectorized element). This plane-width candidate achieves a comparable class of change with a single dict lookup and one float multiply, leaving the vectorized path intact. Where a sampler policy can be expressed as a scale applied to the existing vectorized quantile call, it should be, since the 1,785 s ceiling leaves only ~233 s of headroom over the 1,551.5 s control.

6. Provenance constraints reconfirmed, unchanged: sampler changes must live in data/preprocessing/sampling.py or another module listed in identity.PREPROCESSING_SOURCES; data/triplet_sampling/ is outside the hash; any SAMPLING_IDENTITY edit makes preprocessing_identity differ, so such comparisons need --allowed-input-identity-differences preprocessing_identity as a standing expectation. The empty triplet_sampling/ directory left by the previous candidate has been cleaned up in this workspace and is confirmed absent.

7. Evidence-provenance caveat specific to this candidate, extending the standing limitation in finding 6 of the proposals event: PLANE_SIGMA_RATIO encodes cohort acquisition geometry measured on the fold-0 validation cohort and never re-measured on the training cohort. It is a non-label, fold-agnostic acquisition statistic and is precedent-consistent with how 0.867 was anchored, so it is not leakage, but the realized per-plane widths on the training cohort are an assumption of the derivation and must be read back from the full-schedule CPU replay rather than assumed from the docs' figures.

8. Campaign ownership, carried forward unchanged: both imported ideas are owned by this campaign; docs/backlog/untried-ideas.md entries (including the "Per-plane width ratios" entry with its superseded two-arm axial-alone/coronal-alone design and its 0.008 bar) are read-only provenance and must not be launched as duplicate general-backlog arms. The 0.008 heuristic remains superseded by the sealed paired-bootstrap acceptance rule, and the two-arm design is deliberately replaced by this single justified policy.

2. The park condition does NOT fire: this is one justified policy, not an enumeration of plane multipliers. I reconstructed the derivation rather than accepting the report's framing. Verified present in the cited source, docs/backlog/untried-ideas.md:188-195: realized widths axial 45.1 / coronal 35.8 / sagittal 39.0 mm under the common-fraction rule, and usable half-reach axial 61.5 / coronal 45.6 / sagittal 51.0 mm, ratio 1.35 : 1.00 : 1.12. Applying the implemented constants gives target widths 45.1*1.04213 = 47.00, 35.8*0.97765 = 35.00, 39.0*1.0 = 39.00, i.e. width ratios 1.343 : 1.000 : 1.114 — the acquisition-geometry ratio, reproduced to within rounding. The single free parameter is the normalizing scale, and it is fixed by preserving the current mean width (mean 39.967 mm => k = 34.553, exact targets 46.65 / 34.55 / 38.70), which is exactly how sigma_fraction 0.867 was itself anchored. Sagittal's ratio of exactly 1.0 is emergent, as the developer claimed: its geometry sits at the group mean. One arm, no sweep, no config knob.

3. Finding the developer did not report — a +0.92% scale drift from rounding. The implementation encodes the doc's rounded targets (47/35/39) rather than the unrounded mean-preserving ones (46.65/34.55/38.70). Per-plane deviation from the clean policy is +0.76% axial, +1.29% coronal, +0.78% sagittal, i.e. a near-uniform inflation: implied mean width 40.33 mm against the incumbent 39.97 mm. This is not a contract breach (widths are not a counted quantity; see point 4) and it is immaterial against the already-measured flatness of common-factor sigma sweeps, so it does not warrant the one allowed repair. It is recorded as an interpretation caveat: the arm is a ratio change plus a ~1% common-scale nudge, not a pure ratio change.

4. Count accounting. `sigma` is consumed only at target_depths (sampling.py:479) and in metadata (sampling.py:456-457); grep confirms no other consumer. geometry_groups, study_budgets, largest_remainder and the linear_sum_assignment supplier/assignment logic are byte-identical to parent_source, so `count = min(budget, len(groups))` (sampling.py:451) is structurally invariant — the change only relocates targets within already-existing discrete candidate slices. This satisfies the zero-tolerance requirement by construction, subject to the supervisor's replay confirming it empirically.

5. Plane keying is correct and closed. `kind` reaches select_triplets as the tuple (kind.plane, kind.fluid_sensitive, kind.fat_suppression) from dataset.py:194, so kind[0] is the plane string; SeriesTypeConfig's Literal["Axial","Coronal","Sagittal"] makes the dict total, and an unknown key raises KeyError rather than silently defaulting. dataset.py is the only production caller (scripts/adhoc/analyze_physical_sampling.py is outside the run path).

6. Validation independence and identity. sigma derives solely from pool geometry (limiting_reach) and a literal dict evaluated at import time — no RNG, no training seed, no epoch — so deterministic validation stays seed-independent. The validation percentile path (midpoints, no jitter) is untouched. Deterministic selection does change relative to the incumbent, which is expected and permitted for a shape change; the new selection_sha256 must be recorded explicitly and be stable across all ten validation rows. Training selection remains stochastic across epochs (the rng("targets") jitter is unchanged), so changed_training_studies must stay non-empty.

7. Coverage, exploitation, leakage, cost. Widths move by at most +4.2% (axial) and -2.2% (coronal); the truncation support is set by the pool and is untouched, so no plane collapses toward the centre and no peripheral support is dropped. No label, fold, cohort, grouping, scoring or checkpoint-selection code is touched; no validation-derived feedback; no persistent precomputation. Added cost is one dict lookup and one float multiply per select_triplets call. Critically, and unlike the sibling coronal candidate, this change retains the vectorized truncnorm.ppf — it adds no Python-loop ppf — so the ~22 s CPU exposure measured in the previous review does not apply and runtime headroom against the 1,785 s ceiling is effectively unchanged from the 1,551.5 s control.

Note that this also means the constants encode cohort acquisition geometry measured on fold-0 data. That is a non-label, fold-agnostic acquisition statistic and is precedent-consistent (0.867 was anchored the same way), so it is not leakage, but it should be stated when the result is interpreted.

## completion-20260919T180848Z-c9a551b8

Durable findings from the coronal-side-relative-draw completion, for prior-work.md.

1. RESULT OF RECORD. coronal-side-relative-draw (two-piece normal for physical Coronal pools; per-side sigma from that side's own union-support reach; mass allocated proportional to each side's sigma) is INCONCLUSIVE and the incumbent is retained. Reference historical-control macro AUC 0.8940374, candidate 0.8927278, delta -0.0012969, paired report-group bootstrap 95% interval [-0.0051193, +0.0023229], 852 groups, 2000/2000 valid draws, resampling seed 20260914, training seed 1975885194, candidate epoch 10 vs reference epoch 9. Evidence: experiments/coronal-side-relative-draw-20260919T172815Z-19b2910a/comparison/comparison.json. Interpretation limit recorded at review still applies: this tests REACH-PROPORTIONAL allocation specifically, which moves mass slightly anterior (posterior target mass 0.465 -> 0.436 on the representative pool), not asymmetric coronal sampling in general.

2. NEW AND MOST USEFUL FINDING - shape changes move the model a lot and the metric not at all. The candidate's predictions differ substantially from the incumbent's (mean absolute probability difference 0.0667; per-target Spearman 0.878 Baker's / 0.887 Fracture / 0.902 ACL up to 0.964 Medial OA), yet macro AUC moved -0.0013 with an interval straddling zero. Combined with the settled flatness of the common-factor sigma sweeps, the campaign now has two independent demonstrations that repositioning targets within the existing candidate geometry perturbs the fitted model materially while leaving fold-0 macro AUC flat. Working conclusion for ranking future ideas: the marginal value of further draw-SHAPE work is low, and mechanisms that change the CONTENT of the scored bag (which distinct anatomy appears), its cross-epoch coverage, or what a triplet IS should be preferred over mechanisms that reshape a distribution over an unchanged candidate set.

3. PER-TARGET DIAGNOSTICS (informative only; they do not replace the primary metric and none is individually resolvable at this sample size). Candidate minus reference: Medial Meniscus +0.0097, Synovitis +0.0088, Baker's +0.0082, Effusion +0.0021, PF OA +0.0018; Fracture -0.0010, Lateral OA -0.0024, Contusion -0.0025, Medial OA -0.0050, MCL -0.0109, Lateral Meniscus -0.0112, ACL -0.0131. Family bootstrap means: oa -0.0038 [-0.0116, +0.0038], other_eight -0.0008 [-0.0059, +0.0039], meniscus -0.0007 [-0.0090, +0.0074]; margin variants are far wider (oa_margin -0.0196 [-0.0567, +0.0148]) and should not be read as signal. The pattern worth recording is that the three ligament/meniscal-tear-like targets that most depend on crisp coronal cruciate and collateral depiction (ACL, MCL, Lateral Meniscus) all moved down together, while fluid-sensitive/effusion-adjacent targets moved up - the opposite direction from the anterior mass shift the implementation actually applies. This is the most plausible mechanistic reading of a null macro result: the intervention traded ligament exposure for fluid-collection exposure at roughly zero net. It is a hypothesis generated post hoc from one run and must not be used to justify a targeted retune of the same idea.

4. RUNTIME MODEL VALIDATED - the per-element ppf penalty shows up nearly 1:1 in wall clock. The review predicted ~22 s of extra CPU from replacing the vectorized truncnorm.ppf with a scalar Python loop over ~290k coronal calls, and estimated 1-2 s wall after spreading across 16 dataloader workers. The measured total was 1576.12 s against the historical control's 1551.55 s, i.e. +24.6 s - essentially the full CPU estimate, not the worker-amortized one. Correct the earlier amortization assumption: sampler CPU added inside the dataloader is NOT hidden by worker parallelism in this pipeline. The 0.081 ms/call scalar vs 0.0034 ms/element vectorized benchmark therefore converts directly into wall clock, and against only 233 s of headroom over the 1,785 s ceiling a change of this kind touching all three planes (~3x, ~70 s) would be material. Standing design preference, now evidence-backed: express a sampler policy as a scale or offset applied to the existing vectorized quantile call wherever possible.

5. CONTRACT MECHANICS CONFIRMED END TO END on a real candidate, reusable as the template. A sigma-only change passed the zero-tolerance count gates by construction (sigma is consumed solely by target_depths and the two metadata fields; geometry_groups, study_budgets, largest_remainder and the linear_sum_assignment stage were byte-identical); allowed config differences reduced exactly to the sealed comparison_keys name / paths.output_root / seed; allowed input identity differences reduced exactly to preprocessing_identity, as predicted for any SAMPLING_IDENTITY edit. The comparison tool's own recorded limitations remain the honest frame for every acceptance in this campaign: one training seed, one development fold, intervals exclude training-seed and checkpoint-selection variability, and the allowed configuration and identity differences move together so their separate contributions are not identifiable.

6. NOT TO BE REQUEUED. coronal-side-relative-draw is closed as an implementation. A posterior-favouring side-mass normalization with the same two-sided widths remains formally untested, but it must not be spawned as arm two of this idea; if it ever returns it enters as an independent proposal with its own justification and its own full-budget measurement. All previously recorded closed directions stand unchanged: per-type centre offsets, common-factor sigma sweeps, fixed-total reallocations, repeated_series_share, side-conditioned sagittal sampling, intra-pool overlap/repulsion, and capacity-recovery / shortfall reallocation.

7. Campaign ownership and acceptance rule carried forward unchanged: both imported ideas are owned by this campaign; docs/backlog/untried-ideas.md entries are read-only provenance and must not be launched as duplicate general-backlog arms; the old 0.003/0.008 single-run heuristics remain superseded by the sealed paired-bootstrap rule.

## review-20260919T181739Z-2ba29d77

Durable findings from this review, for prior-work.md.

1. THE PARITY SPLIT IS EXACT AND INCUMBENT-DEGENERATING, VERIFIED BY EXECUTION. The implementation is `lattice = 2*count if band_role is not None else count` with `bands = 2*arange(count) + band_role`. Executed with numpy at n=5: role 0 yields {0.05,0.25,0.45,0.65,0.85}, role 1 {0.15,0.35,0.55,0.75,0.95}; disjoint, minimum cross-role separation exactly 0.5/n, sorted union identical to `(arange(2n)+0.5)/(2n)`. With band_role=None it equals `(arange(n)+0.5)/n` bit-for-bit. Reusable verification idiom, now applied twice in this campaign (the coronal split-normal degenerated to the incumbent when the two side widths were set equal; this one degenerates when band_role is None): every per-slot or per-side policy should expose an explicit parameter value at which it provably collapses onto the incumbent, and that identity should be checked numerically rather than argued.

2. UNEQUAL PARTNER BUDGETS DEGRADE GRACEFULLY, NOT SILENTLY. The parity construction only produces a complementary 2n lattice when both slots realize the same `count`. When they differ (count = min(budget, len(groups)), so capacity-limited pools diverge), role 0 draws from a 2n grid and role 1 from a 2m grid: each slot still spans the full support, and role 1 is effectively offset by half a band relative to role 0. This is the "offset the second slot by half a band" fallback the idea named as its alternative, reached automatically. Worth recording because the union-preservation claim in the developer's report is conditional on equal counts and is only approximate in the capacity-limited minority.

3. THE PER-SLOT MARGINAL DOES SHIFT, AND IN OPPOSITE DIRECTIONS BY ROLE. Deterministically, role 0 sits at (i+0.25)/n and role 1 at (i+0.75)/n against the incumbent (i+0.5)/n, i.e. a systematic +/-0.25/n percentile displacement per slot, and under training jitter each role is confined to its own half of every incumbent band. Only the plane AGGREGATE is (approximately) the incumbent density. Consequence for interpretation: this arm does not merely decorrelate two slots, it also gives the role-0 slot a systematically lower-percentile bag and the role-1 slot a higher-percentile one, with role fixed by config order. Since config order pairs fluid_sensitive=0 as role 0 and fluid_sensitive=1 as role 1 for every plane, any per-target movement will be confounded with a contrast-conditioned depth shift. Note the exact-aggregate property additionally requires the two slots to share support and sigma; they are near-identical in practice but not equal, so aggregate preservation is approximate.

4. SELECTION IDENTITY IS INSULATED FROM SAMPLING METADATA, VERIFIED. accounting.py:78 computes selection_sha256 from the sorted (series_type_index, selected paths) rows and membership from selected paths; neither reads selection.metadata. Therefore new diagnostic metadata keys (here "band_role") are free to add and cannot perturb the recorded selection identity. Complementary fact confirmed in the same pass: TripletLedger.observe derives the counted `triplets` from `batch["slice_mask"].sum(dim=1)` on real batches, so the zero-tolerance gate measures real selections with padding excluded, exactly as the contract requires. gates.py:112 independently enforces a single selection_sha256 across all validation rows, so the "stable across ten validation rows" condition is machine-checked rather than trusted.

5. THE RUNTIME DESIGN PREFERENCE IS NOW OPERATIONAL AND PAID OFF. The standing preference recorded after the coronal completion - express a sampler policy as a scale, offset or index transformation applied to the EXISTING vectorized quantile call, never as a per-element Python loop - was satisfiable for this mechanism: cross-slot interleaving is expressed entirely as an integer index/denominator change upstream of an untouched vectorized truncnorm.ppf, costing one dict lookup and two short np.arange calls. Against the coronal candidate's measured +24.6 s (which consumed the full CPU estimate rather than the worker-amortized one) this is effectively free. Two of three shape/selection mechanisms attempted so far have admitted a vectorized formulation; the preference should be treated as a design requirement for future candidates, with a per-element loop needing explicit budget justification against the ~233 s headroom.

6. PARTNER-PRESENCE PLUMBING PATTERN, REUSABLE. Cross-slot coupling needs a signal that is known before the per-slot loop and identical in both phases. `_slot_sources` (dataset.py:164) is computed for all slots up front and returns (candidates, sources) where `sources` is the list of successfully indexed series; its truthiness is the correct partner-presence test and is config- and data-derived only. Caveat recorded with it: it is evaluated BEFORE `_prepare_slot`'s retry loop, so a partner whose pool later degenerates to empty or non-physical still leaves this slot interleaved. Any future cross-slot mechanism inherits this same boundary, and it is the natural place to tighten if a partner-pool-quality condition is ever wanted.

7. STANDING ITEMS CARRIED FORWARD UNCHANGED. Sampler changes must live in modules listed in identity.PREPROCESSING_SOURCES (data/preprocessing/*.py and data/dataset.py are in; data/triplet_sampling/ is out); any SAMPLING_IDENTITY edit makes preprocessing_identity differ, so such comparisons need --allowed-input-identity-differences preprocessing_identity as a standing expectation. Realized depth histograms from the CPU replay, never target distributions, are the evidence for any shape- or coverage-motivated candidate, because geometry_groups plus linear_sum_assignment snap targets onto existing discrete candidate slices. All cache-derived motivating statistics in this campaign come from the fold-0 VALIDATION cohort and have never been re-measured on the training cohort. Both imported ideas are owned by this campaign; docs/backlog/untried-ideas.md entries are read-only provenance and must not be launched as duplicate general-backlog arms. The old 0.003/0.008 single-run heuristics remain superseded by the sealed paired-bootstrap rule.

APPROVAL BASIS (independent verification, not the author's assertion).

1. Scope. `diff -rq` against parent_source (baseline/source) shows exactly two differing files: src/knee_abnormality/data/dataset.py and src/knee_abnormality/data/preprocessing/sampling.py. Contrary to the developer's report, an immutable source/ snapshot DOES exist and `diff -rq source workspace` is empty, so the snapshot is complete and reviewable. No config or CLI keys were added; configs/training.yaml is unchanged and still carries the pre-existing seed 2583306572, which the supervisor must replace with a fresh drawn seed. Both changed files are in identity.PREPROCESSING_SOURCES, so provenance fidelity holds.

2. Mechanism verified by execution, not inspection. The implementation is `lattice = 2*count`, `bands = 2*arange(count) + band_role`. Executed with the venv numpy: at n=5 role 0 gives percentiles {0.05,0.25,0.45,0.65,0.85} and role 1 {0.15,0.35,0.55,0.75,0.95}; the sets are disjoint, minimum cross-role separation is exactly 0.5/n, and their sorted union equals `(arange(2n)+0.5)/(2n)` exactly. So it is a true parity split of a shared 2n-band lattice, not an approximation. With band_role=None it reproduces `(arange(count)+0.5)/count` bit-for-bit, so single-slot planes, absent partners, and non-pair planes keep the incumbent lattice exactly. Under training jitter each role occupies its own half of each incumbent band, which is the intended per-slot marginal change with the plane aggregate preserved. Unequal partner counts (n != m) degrade gracefully: each slot still spans the full support and role 1 is offset by half a band, which is the documented fallback rather than a failure mode.

3. Count accounting, structurally preserved. `count = min(budget, len(groups))` (sampling.py:454) is untouched and `percentiles` retains length `count`; band_role only changes the percentile values fed to `target_depths`. geometry_groups, union_support, study_budgets, largest_remainder and the entire supplier/linear_sum_assignment stage are byte-identical to parent_source. Independently confirmed that the gate measures real selections: TripletLedger.observe derives `triplets` from `batch["slice_mask"].sum(dim=1)` on actual batches, so padding is excluded and the zero-tolerance gate is satisfied by construction, subject to the supervisor's replay confirming it empirically.

4. Validation independence from the training seed. band_role is produced by `_plane_band_roles(data.series_types)` at __init__ (config order only; first occurrence of a plane = role 0) and is downgraded to None per study solely on the truthiness of the partner's pre-computed `_slot_sources[partner][1]` list of decodable indexed series. Both inputs are data- and config-derived, never RNG- or seed-derived, and the validation path still uses jitter 0.5. Deterministic validation therefore remains seed-independent. Training selection remains stochastic (rng("targets") unchanged, and kind already includes fluid_sensitive so the two slots keep distinct streams), so changed_training_studies must stay non-empty.

5. Selection identity and metadata. Deterministic selection DOES change relative to the incumbent, which is expected and permitted. Verified that the new "band_role" metadata key cannot contaminate the identity: accounting.py:78 computes selection_sha256 from the sorted (series_type_index, selected paths) rows, and membership from selected paths, neither of which reads selection.metadata. gates.py:112 already enforces a single selection_sha256 across the validation rows, so the stability requirement is machine-checked at launch.

6. Leakage, coverage, exploitation, cost. No label, fold, cohort, grouping, scoring or checkpoint-selection code is touched; no validation-derived feedback; no persistent precomputation or extra decode pass. Each slot keeps its own pool, support, sigma and budget, so no pool loses peripheral or central support and the plane's aggregate density is the incumbent truncated Gaussian. Critically for the ceiling: the vectorized `truncnorm.ppf` call inside target_depths is untouched — no Python-loop ppf is introduced — so the runtime model validated on the coronal candidate (+24.6 s measured for ~290k scalar ppf calls, NOT amortized by dataloader workers) does not apply here. Added cost is one dict lookup, one truthiness test and two short np.arange operations per select_triplets call, comfortably inside the ~233 s headroom.
